Mailbox Unit

1. Overview

The Mailbox Unit connects two software endpoints through a pair of FIFOs. Each endpoint has a register aperture: writing WRITE_DATA sends one word to the peer, and reading READ_DATA consumes one word sent by the peer. A single AXI4-Lite slave port serves all pairs.

NUM_MAILBOXES counts bidirectional pairs, each with two directional apertures and two interrupt outputs. The SMC instance provides 32 pairs (64 apertures); the SEP instance provides 8 pairs (16 apertures). Aperture names describe intended use; both ends remain reachable through the same slave port. Access isolation is the subsystem’s responsibility, not a property conferred by naming an aperture inbound or outbound.

Figure 1. SMC mailbox wrapper and interrupt routing

Figure 1 shows the SMC instance of the mailbox, u_smc_axil_mailbox, and its interrupt connections. The SMC configures 32 mailboxes of depth 2, with 32-bit address and 64-bit data. The port comes from the mailbox target of smc_internal_axi_lite_xbar, and the unit runs on a copy of the SMC clock that u_mailbox_cg gates while the port is idle. The demux u_mailbox_demux, a PULP axi_lite_demux, selects one of 64 ports from address bits [16:11] above 0xC001_8000. Each mailbox, gen_mailbox[m].u_axi_lite_mailbox, is a PULP axi_lite_mailbox; the figure puts the source project in parentheses. Mailbox m places its outbound port at 0xC001_8000 + m × 0x1000 and its inbound port 0x800 above it.

Within a mailbox, the arrows between the ports show the direction of data: WRITE_DATA in one port pushes the FIFO that READ_DATA in the other port pops. The 32 inbound-port interrupts are SMC CPU interrupts 288 to 319. The 32 outbound-port interrupts leave the SMC on smc_ext_mailbox_interrupts_o, which the SMU passes out of its boundary.

The SEP holds a second instance of this unit with eight mailboxes, described in the SEP fabric chapter. The outbound interrupts of that instance reach the SMC as peripheral interrupts 0 to 7, SMC CPU interrupts 256 to 263.

2. Architecture

The parameters and ports of axi_lite_mailbox_unit are listed in axi_lite_mailbox_unit.

2.1. Paired FIFOs

An axi_lite_demux selects one of 2 * NUM_MAILBOXES register apertures. Within pair N, the outbound aperture writes FIFO A and reads FIFO B; the inbound aperture writes FIFO B and reads FIFO A. The FIFOs are internal to the unit. Flushing an aperture’s write FIFO therefore discards data its peer would read; flushing its read FIFO discards data the peer has already sent.

WRITE_DATA enqueues one word when space is available. Inactive AXI byte lanes are written as zero; a partial write does not merge with an existing FIFO word. READ_DATA returns and pops the oldest received word. Software must use status to avoid full writes and empty reads; the interface reports errors rather than waiting for FIFO space or data. The error responses are described below.

2.2. Interrupts

Each aperture has its own WIRQT, RIRQT, IRQS, IRQEN and IRQP. inbound_interrupt_o[N] and outbound_interrupt_o[N] are active-high level outputs. Each is the OR of IRQP = IRQS & IRQEN for its aperture.

Table 1. Mailbox interrupt sources
IRQS bit Source Set condition

0

Write threshold

Occupancy of the aperture’s write FIFO is strictly greater than WIRQT. This indicates queued outbound data, not successful peer receipt.

1

Read threshold

Occupancy of its read FIFO is strictly greater than RIRQT.

2

Access error

A full-FIFO write or empty-FIFO read. ERROR_FLAGS distinguishes which access failed.

These status bits latch independently of IRQEN. Acknowledge selected bits by writing ones to IRQS; reading it does not clear them. Threshold status can reassert if the occupancy condition remains true. WIRQT and RIRQT writes are clamped to MAILBOX_DEPTH - 1, so a threshold of zero detects any queued word and the maximum threshold detects a full FIFO.

STATUS exposes live FIFO-empty/full and threshold conditions. It is distinct from the latched IRQS. Reading ERROR_FLAGS clears the detailed error flags, but does not acknowledge the shared IRQS error bit.

2.3. Clock and Reset

The unit and its FIFOs use clk_i; there is no internal clock-domain crossing. rst_ni is asynchronous and active low. Reset empties the FIFOs and clears thresholds, error flags, interrupt status and enables. test_en_i supplies the AXI demultiplexer’s test input. Follow subsystem reset and access-isolation requirements when either endpoint is active.

3. Interface and Programming

3.1. External Interface

A single AXI4-Lite slave (mailbox_axi_req_i / mailbox_axi_resp_o) serves all apertures; its address/data widths and channel/aggregate struct types are elaboration parameters. The inbound_interrupt_o and outbound_interrupt_o vectors each carry NUM_MAILBOXES active-high level interrupts, one per aperture.

The full parameter and port list of axi_lite_mailbox_unit is in axi_lite_mailbox_unit.

The per-aperture B and R responses pass through spill registers. Request arbitration, arrival of both write channels, and response backpressure also affect observed latency; software must not assume a fixed completion cycle. Reads and writes use separate AXI4-Lite channels but operate on the same FIFO and status state.

3.2. Access Results

Table 2. Mailbox access results
Access Result

Read READ_DATA while empty

SLVERR, data 0xFEEDDEAD; sets the read-error flag and IRQS[2].

Write WRITE_DATA while full

SLVERR; data discarded; sets the write-error flag and IRQS[2].

Read write-only WRITE_DATA

OKAY, data 0xFEEDC0DE; no FIFO access or error flag.

Write READ_DATA, STATUS, ERROR_FLAGS or IRQP

Ignored with SLVERR.

Access to an unmapped offset inside an aperture (0x50 and above)

Stated in the SMC and SEP memory maps

Read ERROR_FLAGS

Returns and clears both detailed error flags. A new error in the same cycle is retained.

Write CTRL flush bits

Pulses the selected FIFO flush; the bits are not stored configuration and read back as zero.

3.3. Sending and Receiving

The register interface does not allocate FIFO ownership to clients, and status and data accesses are not one atomic operation. The Programmer’s Guide gives the mailbox usage rules.

4. Memory Map and Register Reference

4.1. Memory Map

With the default MAILBOX_SIZE=0x800, each directional aperture occupies 2 KiB and each pair occupies 4 KiB. The SMC 32-pair map spans 0x20000 bytes, and the SEP 8-pair map spans 0x8000 bytes; the generic unit’s span is 2 * MAILBOX_SIZE * NUM_MAILBOXES. The SMC and SEP wrapper layouts are generated from their SystemRDL compositions:

4.1.1. Per-Mailbox Register Map

Each aperture contains ten registers. Offsets are spaced by DATA_WIDTH/8, so they are 8 bytes apart in the 64-bit configurations used today. The generated detailed register map below is authoritative for offsets, widths, access, and field descriptions. The CTRL flush bits are pulses rather than stored state, so reads return zero with an OKAY response.

The SMC and SEP memory maps state how an offset above the registers answers.

4.1.2. Address Examples

For mailbox pair N, the outbound aperture begins at BASE + N * 0x1000; the inbound aperture begins 0x800 bytes later. A register address is the aperture base plus the generated register offset. For example, in pair zero, READ_DATA is at BASE + 0x8 outbound and BASE + 0x808 inbound.

4.2. Detailed Register Map

AXI-Lite Mailbox Address Map

Register interface for a single AXI-Lite mailbox: data FIFOs, level and status flags, and interrupt controls.

Register List:

0x38IRQENRWThis register is used to enable and disable which interrupts are sent to the CPU.

READ_DATA

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription

STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:4Reserved--Reserved
3read_level_above_threshR0x0If set, read fifo level is higher than threshold set in RIRQT
2write_level_above_threshR0x0If set, write fifo level is higher than threshold set in WIRQT
1fullR0x00: Space is available to write, 1: Space is not available to write
0emptyR0x10: Data is available to read, 1: Data is not available to read

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:2Reserved--Reserved
1write_errorR RC0x0Attempted write to a full mailbox
0read_errorR RC0x0Attempted read from an empty mailbox

WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

RIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0rirqtRW0x0When the fill pointer of the FIFO connected to the R channel exceeds this value, a read threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the read FIFO is full.

4.2.1. Subsystem Wrapper Maps

The wrapper maps describe the generated SMC and SEP mailbox compositions. Their offsets are relative to the corresponding wrapper aperture.

SMC AXI-Lite Mailbox Wrapper Address Map

SMC-side wrapper instantiating the AXI-Lite mailbox pairs.
Each outbound_mailbox_N / inbound_mailbox_N pair is the two apertures of one
bidirectional mailbox and shares a single pair of FIFOs: data written through
WRITE_DATA in one aperture is what READ_DATA in the other aperture returns.
The outbound/inbound naming reflects intended use, not an access restriction.

Register Details:

outbound_mailbox_0.WRITE_DATA

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:0write_dataW WMOD0x0Write data to mailbox fifo

outbound_mailbox_0.READ_DATA

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:0read_dataR RMOD-Read data from mailbox fifo

outbound_mailbox_0.STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:4Reserved--Reserved
3read_level_above_threshR0x0If set, read fifo level is higher than threshold set in RIRQT
2write_level_above_threshR0x0If set, write fifo level is higher than threshold set in WIRQT
1fullR0x00: Space is available to write, 1: Space is not available to write
0emptyR0x10: Data is available to read, 1: Data is not available to read

outbound_mailbox_0.ERROR_FLAGS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:2Reserved--Reserved
1write_errorR RC0x0Attempted write to a full mailbox
0read_errorR RC0x0Attempted read from an empty mailbox

outbound_mailbox_0.WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

outbound_mailbox_0.RIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0rirqtRW0x0When the fill pointer of the FIFO connected to the R channel exceeds this value, a read threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the read FIFO is full.

outbound_mailbox_0.IRQS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:3Reserved--Reserved
2eirqRW1C0x0On read:
[0]: No interrupt request
[1]: Error on mailbox access
On write:
[0]: No acknowledge
[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request
1rtirqRW1C0x0On read:
[0]: No interrupt request
[1]: Usage level threshold in read mailbox exceeded
On write:
[0]: No acknowledge
[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request
0wtirqRW1C0x0On read:
[0]: No interrupt request
[1]: Usage level threshold in write mailbox exceeded
On write:
[0]: No acknowledge
[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request

outbound_mailbox_0.IRQEN

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:3Reserved--Reserved
2eirqRW0x0[0]: Error IRQ disabled
[1]: Error IRQ enabled
1rtirqRW0x0[0]: Read threshold IRQ disabled
[1]: Read threshold IRQ enabled
0wtirqRW0x0[0]: Write threshold IRQ disabled
[1]: Write threshold IRQ enabled

outbound_mailbox_0.IRQP

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription

[1]: Read threshold IRQ pending
0wtirqR0x0[0]: No write threshold IRQ pending
[1]: Write threshold IRQ pending

outbound_mailbox_13.WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

SEP AXI-Lite Mailbox Wrapper Address Map

SEP-side wrapper instantiating the AXI-Lite mailbox pairs.
Each outbound_mailbox_N / inbound_mailbox_N pair is the two apertures of one
bidirectional mailbox and shares a single pair of FIFOs: data written through
WRITE_DATA in one aperture is what READ_DATA in the other aperture returns.
The outbound/inbound naming reflects intended use, not an access restriction.

Register List:

AddressNameAccessDescription
0x0outbound_mailbox_0.WRITE_DATAW-
0x8outbound_mailbox_0.READ_DATAR-
0x10outbound_mailbox_0.STATUSR-
0x18outbound_mailbox_0.ERROR_FLAGSR-
0x20outbound_mailbox_0.WIRQTRW-
0x28outbound_mailbox_0.RIRQTRW-
0x30outbound_mailbox_0.IRQSRWThis register is used to read and clear interrupt requests, regardless of IRQEN register and whether the interrupt is enabled or disabled.
0x38outbound_mailbox_0.IRQENRWThis register is used to enable and disable which interrupts are sent to the CPU.
0x40outbound_mailbox_0.IRQPRThis register is used to read the current status of the interrupts sent to the CPU.
0x48outbound_mailbox_0.CTRLW-

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:4Reserved--Reserved
3read_level_above_threshR0x0If set, read fifo level is higher than threshold set in RIRQT
2write_level_above_threshR0x0If set, write fifo level is higher than threshold set in WIRQT
1fullR0x00: Space is available to write, 1: Space is not available to write
0emptyR0x10: Data is available to read, 1: Data is not available to read

outbound_mailbox_0.WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

outbound_mailbox_0.RIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0rirqtRW0x0When the fill pointer of the FIFO connected to the R channel exceeds this value, a read threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the read FIFO is full.

outbound_mailbox_7.WIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0wirqtRW0x0When the usage pointer of the FIFO connected to the W channel exceeds this value, a write threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the write FIFO is full.

outbound_mailbox_7.RIRQT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:8Reserved--Reserved
7:0rirqtRW0x0When the fill pointer of the FIFO connected to the R channel exceeds this value, a read threshold IRQ is triggered and the corresponding STATUS register bit is set. When a value larger than or equal to the MailboxDepth parameter is written to this register, it gets reduced to MailboxDepth - 1 to ensure an IRQ is triggered when the read FIFO is full.

[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request

[1]: Usage level threshold in read mailbox exceeded

[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request

[0]: No interrupt request
[1]: Usage level threshold in write mailbox exceeded

[1]: Acknowledge and clear interrupt request

inbound_mailbox_7.STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
63:4Reserved--Reserved
3read_level_above_threshR0x0If set, read fifo level is higher than threshold set in RIRQT
2write_level_above_threshR0x0If set, write fifo level is higher than threshold set in WIRQT
1fullR0x00: Space is available to write, 1: Space is not available to write
0emptyR0x10: Data is available to read, 1: Data is not available to read
